# Findings ledger — Goose MLX quality loop

Method and statuses: `~/.claude/skills/goose-mlx-quality-loop/SKILL.md`. Ids are assigned by the coordinator;
rows are never deleted. Severity: dead end > misleads > friction > cosmetic. Verdict: CONFIRMED (seen on
screen / measured) or PLAUSIBLE (read in code).

Reconciled 2026-09-27: every non-terminal status re-derived from the code. "shipped <v>" = the first release whose
checkout contains the sha (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`): v3.0.35/v3.0.39 tags, then the goose-rel checkout reflog
against ~/goose-builds/release-3.0.NN.log (3.0.40 d6ee39ebc · .41 76a3cbf41 · .42 13e7e3af2 · .43 b76139db6 · .44

Engine. · 2026-09-27 a8c329084: sessions.db read (read-only) — 20260926_1..20 all still `"context_limit":128000`. Cause was general, not swarm: `model_info_for_provider_model` gave every undeclared model DEFAULT_CONTEXT_LIMIT and `normalize_model_config` saved it as if declared (it also stopped openai-compat/litellm from probing). Now 0 = unknown, nothing saved; swarm chat's `get_context_limit` = last pick's pool window, else measured now by the pick's probes (no slot), else an error naming why; `effective_context_limit` → Option (unknown: MOIM says so, no proactive compaction/autoload, /status "window unknown (…)", CLI "context usage unavailable"). PROVE: a new swarm chat on a build with a8c329084 → sessions.db model_config_json has `"context_limit":null`, and the first turn's log shows the pool's window (262,144) with no `context_window_unknown` warn. |

UI-only. · fixed 2026-09-27 dbddaf9c6: NOT a code regression — the new test is red on 4333464ef's own ChatInput; that fix held the limit only for readiness reconnecting (a route), and a dead split reports contextWindow null → limit 0. heldContextLimit (chatServedBy/contextFloor.ts) keeps the last window measured for this chat + provider:model while no engine reports one; another chat/model gets none. Test ChatInput.localEngine.test.tsx. Not walked live (needs a split to die). PROVE: kill a split rank mid-chat — "N / 262k" stays under the bar. |

Engine (Rapid-MLX fork scheduler), not UI. · FIX 2026-09-27 (fork f0a3cd07b, branch lz/single-srpf, tag v0.14.3-lz.10; goose 213eb38c3 pins it, lz.9 superseded so the owner's persisted default migrates; pipeline review a18e14fd4 = tag lz-pipeline-qwen4.12, JSON-only): Q-145/Q-160's order on the ONE-row engine, never two rows in a batch — at a prefill chunk boundary a waiting request with fewer prompt tokens left takes the engine and the prefilling row is PARKED (its cache comes back as the prefix-hit shape and resumes exactly); jumper-only half-slack bound aged in measured engine seconds (a jumper also decodes before the parked row resumes); a park needs active + parked copy + the jumper at its horizon < cap, priced from the live row's buffers (Q-110 kept closed); decode stays batch-1 MTP, singleton caches. `serve --singleton-prefill-order srpf|fifo` (default srpf; fifo = lz.9 for the A/B), `/v1/status singleton_prefill_order` {parks, refused, prefill_seconds_per_token, parked[]}. CPU only (real Scheduler + BatchGenerator + vendored MTP on a tiny qwen3_5 hybrid): 3-token request's first token 13 steps after arrival on lz.9 → 2; answers identical to solo; every-step stream: long first token 17 vs 9 solo (control without the bound: starved). Residuals: arrival during a DECODE waits that decode; `kv_cache` int8/int4 refuses parks (FIFO). Fix (tag lz-pipeline-qwen4.5, on .4): one tick loop on every rank — a decode step, then (granted by rank 0) one --prefill-step chunk of the ONE joining request, which prefills in its own cache (solo chunk edges), samples its first token and merges into the batch; admission = a free slot + `_KvBudget` fits every rank, FIFO, broadcast as a plan the step's collective announces; chunks spaced so decode and prefill share measured time equally (share decides WHEN, never admission or chunk size — Q-103's shrunken chunks cost −67%). 14:39 trace: the 39,194-token head is admitted at the next decode step; the canary still waits for a slot behind two 39k requests (slots 2, full-context reservations). Decode cost, synthetic model in process: running row −46% while a 4k joiner prefills (TTFT 4.8 s vs 3.9 s idle), batch-2 decode +0%/−42% across two noisy runs; NOT measured on Flash. goose: pipeline_rank.py now measures the fork's `_Engine._start`/`_Engine.prefill` (the old `run_batch` seam is gone — the bare pin bump would have exited every Run at the guard). 2-rank launch tests written, NOT run (a split held this Mac). | Q-145 | 2026-09-26 | performance/STABILITY | split · PIPELINE mode · admission order | Flash pipeline on 3.0.51, load.py (fixed harness 987f8c9f6) 3 workers × ~39k-token unique prompts + a 13-token canary, all at t0: first tokens at 115 / 232 / 347 s and the CANARY's first token at 347 s — every prefill ran one after another and the 1-token request waited behind all three. Decode itself is healthy (max gap 0.67–0.72 s). /v1/status 22:14 during round 2: `slots 2, slots_in_use 1, num_waiting 2` — the one running row is in `phase: prefill` (18,176/39,263 at 361 tok/s) while a 38,880-token request has waited 164 s beside the free slot. In goose that is every helper call (fact checker, title, compaction summary) queued behind the agent's long prefill (the Q-134 14:39 trace saw "a goose turn waited behind goose's own fact checker"). | LOAD-2026-09-26c-flash-pipeline requests.csv + /v1/status | CONFIRMED | PROVEN LIVE load 26e (3.0.56, fork .11): steady-state canaries 1.94 / 1.98 / 2.73 / 1.50 s first token under 3×30k load (26d: 353 / 297 s; 26c: 347 s); long TTFTs 94 / 191 / 285 s (serial prefill, as designed); decode gaps ~1.0 s | Q-134's tick loop admits ONE joiner at a time and only between decode steps, FIFO; a request whose whole prompt fits in one prefill chunk still queues behind every earlier long prefill. Serial prefill itself is right (prefill is compute-bound, running two in parallel buys no throughput); the defect is the ORDER. Design: between chunks of a long prefill, a queued request whose remaining prefill is shorter than the running one's remaining (shortest-remaining-prefill-first, with an aging bound so a long request can't starve) joins next. Also found: load.py's ttft counted the role-only opening chunk (fixed 987f8c9f6); the earlier 26b rows' ttft are invalid. FIX (fork c24f6b55e): shortest-remaining-prefill-first at chunk granularity — several rows may prefill, each in its own cache and slot; every rank runs the chunk of the row with the fewest prompt tokens left (derived from ranges every rank holds, no new collective word); rank 0 admits the queued head only when it would be that pick; aging in prefill tokens (protected once the tokens prefilled for others reach its own tokens left); Q-134's KV admission and decode/prefill share kept; a finished prefill joins the decode batch at once (unchanged). 26c TRACE: A (39,263) is admitted alone; B/C/canary arrive during A's chunk 1, the canary (13 left vs 35,167) is admitted after A's chunk 2, prefills in one chunk and gets its first token — predicted canary TTFT ~6-12 s (<= 2 A chunks at 341-361 tok/s) instead of 347 s; the three long first tokens stay ~115/232/347 s (slots 2 bind them, not the order); a canary arriving while BOTH slots are held still waits for a row to leave. goose companion (branch q145-pin-companion 190d9be6f): pipeline_rank.py read `state.held`, which the fork removed — it must land WITH the pin bump or every /v1/status raises. LIVE 26d 23:30–23:37: canaries 1–2 first token 3.32 s / 1.48 s (vs 347 s), decode gaps 0.66–0.74 s, long TTFTs 118/239/358 s as predicted — BUT canary 3 (27 tokens) then waited 290+ s FIRST in queue while one 39k request prefilled (/v1/status: running prefill 20,224/39,016; waiting [27, 39,259, 38,849]). Cause (from the agent's own description of the aging bound): a request that has 'waited as many tokens as it still has left' can no longer be jumped, and the wait counts EVERY token processed while it queued — including the other long prefills that were ahead of it FIFO anyway. With 3 workers every long request ages before it starts, so in steady state nothing can jump and SRPF degenerates to FIFO. Fix: count as 'wait' only the prefill tokens of requests that JUMPED it (arrived after it and went first) — the bound then caps the slowdown jumpers cause (≤ 2×), not the queueing it would have had anyway. FIX 2 (fork f8a2461a5 on lz-pipeline-qwen4.9, tag lz-pipeline-qwen4.10; goose pin c22ccae60): a chunk ages only the requests that arrived BEFORE its row; a request's slack = its own prefill − jumpers' tokens − what later rows already admitted will still prefill; a later request may go ahead only by taking ≤ HALF the slack it finds (whole-slack let a 1%-shorter long prompt spend a long one's slack in one jump and block every canary — steady-state sim, 3 seeds: canary max 86/252/151 s whole vs 85 s half). 26d REPLAY (fork test, measured 333 tok/s prefill, 0.123 s/decode token, requests.csv arrivals): canaries 0.1 / 10.7 / 236.9 / 311.3 s on 419306f70 → 0.1 / 10.7 / 4.2 / 11.3 s (chunk-steps 1/3/40/52 → 1/3/1/3); long first tokens unchanged (~355 s steady; slots 2). Behaviour change: near-equal long prompts now keep first-come order (the Q-134 14:39 test's three longs). Residual, unchanged: a canary arriving while BOTH slots are held (one decoding, one prefilling) waits for a row to leave — the only blocker left in the sim. |

Instrument (known design, dispatched now): /v1/status carries the running request's parser state (normal/reasoning/tool/broken + the reason) and the last ~2k chars of generated text. Fix: decided from the words. **instrument 56e1487ba** (branch worktree-agent-a96fabc8ee94cfb8a, not live until a new build): every /v1/status `requests[]` row carries `stream` (null = not a streamed chat answer): `parser_state` (normal/reasoning/tool/stopped), `generated_chars`, `sent_chars`, `since_sent_chars` (generated since the last frame with content), `content_frames`, `withholding` {mode, reason, withheld_chars}, `tool_call` {streamed, name, phase, parameter, string_value, broken, call_chars, sent_chars}, `tail` (last 2,000 chars, control sequences as written); `GOOSE_RANK_WITHHELD` enter/leave lines in the rank log, the leave carrying the words. Note: /v1/status's completion_tokens is counted in the HANDLER thread as it consumes, so #3d's 9,945 means the handler read every token and sent none — NOT socket back-pressure. **Withholding paths, mlx_lm 0.31.3 handle_completion** (→ Q-141 coverage): (1) `tool` state, server.py:1480 `gen.state != "tool"` — whole call held to `</tool_call>` → COVERED for qwen3_coder on streamed chat, except: (1a) another tool parser → NOT (mode tool_not_streamed; the 27B's parser is qwen3_coder, so not #3d); (1b) streamer `broken` (a parameter written twice, a header closed without '>', a typed value the converter refuses) → rest of the call withheld, then dropped (RANK_TOOL_CALL_UNPARSED) → NOT (tool_broken); (1c) text where the qwen3_coder frame should be (JSON inside <tool_call>, prose before <function= or between parameters) → the streamer reads none of it, not even `broken` → NOT (tool_unread); (1d) a non-string parameter value (integer/object/array) → held whole until its `</parameter>` → NOT by design (tool_typed_value) — a huge array/object argument is a 10k-token silence on its own; (1e) a string value's last 13 chars + first 4 ("null" guard) → constant lag, covered by design; (1f) call cut at the end (eos/max_tokens inside a call) → parser refuses, client holds unterminated args → named, not silent. (2) `reasoning` state → NOT withheld: sent per piece as delta `reasoning` (server.py:1357), which goose reads (goose-provider-types openai.rs:231-242). (3) stop/control sequences, `_process_control_tokens` server.py:236 → a lag of the longest sequence in tokens; a matched sequence's text is never sent (by design). (4) streaming detokenizer (tokenizer_utils BPE add_token) holds incomplete UTF-8 / a lone space until the next token; the batch path never calls `finalize()` (server.py:884-887), so a trailing unflushed fragment is DROPPED at the end — tiny, not covered. (5) the stop token (state None) → not accumulated, by design. (6) `stream:false` → whole answer at the end (client's choice; `stream: null`). (7) streamed `/v1/completions` (text_completion) → the Q-141 wrapper applies to chat only, so its tool state is withheld whole → NOT (goose sends chat). (8) prefill → only `: keepalive p/t` comments, no text exists yet. For #3d: (1b), (1c) or (1d); the next /v1/status poll on a build carrying 56e1487ba names which and shows the words. CORRECTION 23:2x (live round 1): the framing "nothing streamed" is WRONG — goose's chat footer read "goose is writing 41 tool calls, the latest to ledger__ledger_append — 18.5k chars of arguments" during the call, so the Q-141 tool-call deltas DO reach goose (its llm_request log only writes at the end). What stands: ~24k tokens generated vs ~18.5k chars (~5k tokens) received — ~80% withheld or never streamable (non-string parameter values are held whole until they close, per the instrument's table), and ONE answer carrying 41+ tool calls over 40 min (a runaway turn). The 3.0.54 instrument will say which. |

| Q-160 | 2026-09-27 | performance/UX | split · PIPELINE mode · slots | Residual of Q-145 named by its fix agent and visible in 26d: when both of the pipeline's slots are in use (slots 2 = the planner's full-context KV reservation per row, launch.rs:370), a short request (a goose helper call: fact checker, title, summary) waits for a whole row to LEAVE — in real use a decode of minutes — though its own KV need is a few hundred tokens. | 26d /v1/status (slots 2, in_use 2 moments) + Q-145 agent report | CONFIRMED (mechanism) · live wait not yet isolated | PROVEN LIVE load 26e (3.0.56): canaries admitted during two decoding rows get their first token in 1.5–2.7 s; no row refused for slots (one 503 was Q-165, memory attribution) | Direction: admission bounded by the MEASURED KV budget per request (prompt + max_tokens actually reserved), not by a slot count sized for two full-context rows; the batch width stays a compute bound. Keep rank-plan consistency and Q-134/Q-145. CAUSE (read at f8a2461a5): slots are NOT preallocated caches — each request prefills in its own cache and is merged into one batch padded to its longest row (mlx-lm BatchKVCache.merge). Two bounds held a helper back: `--max-batch` (goose passed slots=2) and `_KvBudget.reserve` = rows x the longest horizon, each with a chunk workspace; goose's chats send no max_tokens, so each reserved the whole context. In process on f8a2461a5 (tiny model, real _KvBudget, two chats + a 12-token request): first token 451 / 450 / 451 decode steps at max_batch 2 / 3 / 4 — always after a chat finished. FIX: `_KvBudget.held` prices what the rows can hold before anything joins — running rows grow to their horizon (prompt + max_tokens + 1) and leave, so the peak is rows-alive x width at some row's last step (the padding is priced), prefilling rows at their prompts in their own caches, plus the larger of one decode step's or one SOLO chunk's transient (prefill runs alone since Q-134; the plan budgets one chunk per slot); a batch of more rows than the planned slots is also priced with `_regroup`'s one-layer copy, which the planner never budgets. Admission = fits with the row prefilling, with every prefilling row joined now, and with any prefilling row grown to its horizon; the last chunk (the merge) runs only once the batch it makes fits, prefix entries yielding first by a plan. `--max-batch` is gone: the plan header's rows are derived from the budgets on every rank and proven equal. E2E on 7d3327202: 3 decode steps, both chats still decoding, measured cache bytes <= reserved <= budget at every step, worker replay identical. Flash (planner formulas, 2 ranks, slots 2): a 600+256 helper beside two chats fits at ctx 131k/200k/262k x step 2048/1792/256; full-context rows the budget holds: 4/3/2 — a THIRD chat with no max_tokens is now admitted on step-2048 plans (memory priced truthfully; the old double chunk workspace was slack). Residual: a merged buffer can run < 1 KVCache.step per row past the planner's rounding (unchanged, the planner has the same gap). Live prove (26e): two chats decoding + a helper — the helper's first token while both stream, /v1/status sequences_in_flight 3. |
| Q-161 | 2026-09-27 | STABILITY/quality | split (tensor) · runaway answer | E2E #3e turn 0, 3.0.55 (sampling FIXED: /v1/status sampling_defaults temperature 1.0 / top_p 0.95 / top_k 20 from generation_config): goose's first agent call (40,540 in) generated 221,604 tokens over ~6.5 h — ONE answer holding 649 tool calls = the SAME pair (write notes/kickoff.md with identical content + shell `mkdir -p …/notes`) 324 times + 1 truncated call. The Q-159 goose guard held ("Not run: identical to call #1 in this same answer") but cannot stop the generation. Same shape as #3d under greedy (54 identical ledger_append) → greedy was NOT the whole cause. Ruled out: EOS config (mlx_lm load_config merges generation_config eos [248046 <|im_end|>, 248044 <| REOPENED — the ported guard does not hold the shape live (E2E #3f, 3.0.57) · cutting |>] into the sharded load's tokenizer). Not seen on the Studio single engine with the same checkpoint (E2E #4). Suspects: the tensor wrapper's batch/stream path misses an end-of-turn so the model restarts its answer (identical pairs = a restarted answer); the single engine's Q-85 XML tool-call guard (lz.7) absent on the split; no repetition/presence penalty. LIVE 3.0.57 E2E #3f turn 0 (11:52–11:58), sampling = generation_config, GOOSE_RANK_XML_GUARD armed:true on rank 0: goose's forming panel '1 tool call forming — write, 1,692 chars' then 'Text written beside the calls (3,251 chars)': `!\n</parameter>\n</function>\n!\n</parameter>\n</function>\n!…` repeating, never `</tool_call>`; /v1/status stream parser_state 'normal', tool_call None, generated 4,345 / sent 4,308 chars at 1,480 tokens, tail = `! | </function> | ! | </function>…`. So the model leaves the call through a path the ported rules do not constrain (the `!` lands after `</parameter>` / `</function>` and the parser drops back to normal text), and the verbatim-repeat stop never fires because no second call completes. Evidence: RU-2026-09-27-3f-split-tensor/status-runaway-1.json, forming.png, session 20260927_1. Run stopped (composer Stop). | sessions.db 20260926_20 (680 assistant msgs) + calls.csv RU-2026-09-27-3e + rank0 log | CONFIRMED (shape + mechanism, measured live on 8091) | fixed 369a2cac2 (tensor wrapper: the lz.7 XML skeleton guard on every rank + a verbatim-repeat stop on rank 0) · NOT yet proven on the live split: needs a build carrying 369a2cac2 on BOTH Macs (new program tag mlxLmServerSkeletonGuard — an older peer refuses the spec); the guard's effect was emulated live 4/4 | MECHANISM (words, 2026-09-27, 8091, #3e's turn-0 request): the checkpoint does not end its turn after a call — its end-of-turn mass sits on `!` (token id 0). Next-token log-probs after the template's own `</tool_call>`: one call — `!` −0.00, `\n` −4.0, `<|im_end|>` −7.06; after the write+mkdir pair — `!` −0.13, `<|im_end|>` −2.6, `\n` −2.9; after 4/6/8 pairs `\n` then `<tool_call>` p≈1.0, `<|im_end|>` −2.4…−3.3 (each copy makes the next more certain). Live continuation from the pair (no guard): "</tool_call>! 2>/dev/null\necho "done"\n</parameter>\n</function>\n</tool_call>!\n</parameter>\n!" then "</parameter>\n!" to 3,000 tokens, no end token; 1 of 5 plain replays: "</parameter>\n! 2026-09-24 kickoff, Harbourline Jira DC→Cloud" repeated inside a call that never closed (length). #3e took the other branch (`\n<tool_call>`, no text in 649 calls — so no `<|im_end|>` was skipped: EOS {248044, 248046} are in the runtime state machine, and an ignored end would have streamed `<|im_start|>` text). The single engine masks exactly these positions since lz.7 (Q-85); the split had nothing. FIX: rank_xml_guard.py (the lz.7 rules as an mlx_lm logits processor, tool requests only, GOOSE_RANK_XML_GUARD at load) + StreamedToolCalls holds a call while it is word for word an earlier call of the answer and, if it closes identical, never sends it: GOOSE_RANK_TOOL_CALL_REPEATED (with the tail words), ctx.stop(), finish `tool_calls`. Emulation (the shipped guard applied client-side at every armed position): 3/3 turn-0 answers "…</function>\n</tool_call><|im_end|>" after one write, 1/1 from the pair "…</tool_call><|im_end|>" after one ledger_append; 0 positions off the skeleton. Trace caveat: #3e's raw words were never recorded, so the repeat stop firing at call 3 assumes raw = parsed identity — the guard makes that hold. Also: goose sends no max_tokens, so one answer may use the whole remaining window (222,148) — the budget turned a loop into 6.5 h. |

| Q-163 | 2026-09-27 | test-infra (maybe product) | developer shell tool · CI | Two sibling tests failed on CI within a day, each once, each passing on re-run/locally: `developer::tests::developer_client_uses_working_dir_for_shell_tool` (31a457b5b: `pwd` result is_error Some(true)) and `developer::shell::tests::shell_uses_working_dir_for_relative_execution` (4d0abf3da, shell.rs:1336: is_error Some(true)). The shell call itself RETURNED AN ERROR under a loaded runner — and the assertion prints no error text, so the cause is invisible. Two in one family = a pattern, not noise. Could be product: a shell tool call that fails under load for a user too (login-shell probe, Q-102 node shims, or Q-138's orphan reaper hitting a sibling test's child). | CI runs of 31a457b5b + 4d0abf3da | CONFIRMED (twice; reproduced 124/34,759 and 126/27,521 pwd calls) · cause FOUND: test-infra, NOT a product defect | fixed 887f8c1b1 · awaiting live prove | Cause: Q-102's `shell_tool_prefers_the_users_node…` (80bbd6df2, landed hours before both failures) set GOOSE_SHELL=<tmp>/login-shell and PATH in the PROCESS env via env_lock; the pwd tests take no lock, so their `unix_shell()` read the fake shell, and when the Q-102 test ended and deleted its tmp between the exec and /bin/sh opening the script the call returned `/bin/sh: /var/folders/…/T/.tmpXXXX/login-shell: No such file or directory` (exit 127 → is_error). CI logs agree: the Q-102 test printed "ok" 4 ms (4d0abf3da, 21:30:38.9967 → .0007) and 7 ms (31a457b5b attempt 1, 20:13:18.2949 → .3018) before each failure. Alphabetical adjacency on a 4-core runner is why it hit CI and not a 32-thread laptop (0/60, 0/300 with 8× `yes`, 0/100 at 4 threads — the window is ms). Proof: an in-process amplifier looping the real Q-102 test beside real `pwd` calls: before 124 + 126 bad, every one that text; after 0/19,538. The hooks PATH test (Q-140) had the same shape (GOOSE_SHELL to a fake that ignores its args). Fix: both, plus the ignored live probe, now run their env in a CHILD test process (`crates/goose/src/test_env_child.rs::run_ignored_test_in_child`, which refuses unless exactly the one test ran and passed); tripwire `no_in_process_test_sets_what_shell_spawns_read` fails the build on any lock_env/set_var of GOOSE_SHELL/SHELL/PATH/GOOSE_TOOL_SHIM_DIR in crates/goose (negative control: it flags the old shell.rs). Every developer is_error assertion now prints the tool text. Ruled out: login-shell probe (not used by either test), Q-138 reaper (bundled-mcps paths, PPID 1 only), process_groups (reaps only its two sessions' live groups; a live pgid is not reused), temp-dir names (unique). Seen, not the cause: `probe_login_shell_path` still reads with a typed 5 s window (gate 10). · reconciled 2026-09-27: status was «fixed (agent, this branch)». status said 'this branch' — 887f8c1b1 is merged to main (5f28e2cf0) after the 3.0.56 checkout; test-only, so no build carries it. PROVE: CI's developer shell tests green across the next runs. |

| Q-223 | 2026-09-27 | STABILITY | app quit leaves goosed (goose serve) running → Link blocked | Owner screenshot after the 3.0.62 install: Link 'Connect failed — a tailscaled already answers on socket … (listener pid 11275) … stop it per-pid'. Cause, measured: the 3.0.61 goosed pid 10891 (goose serve --tls --platform desktop, started 20:49:26) SURVIVED the app quit as an orphan (ppid 1, 28–37% CPU for 1h22m) and still owned tailscaled 11275, so the new app's Link skipped reconnect ('another goose on this Mac already holds the mesh') and the split could not be offered. Three more orphaned goose serve processes from an agent's packaged build (16:09–16:23, ppid 1) were still alive. Killed per pid 22:11; Retry connect → tailscaled 55892, split up 'OK' | every app quit/update (install.sh quits via osascript) | CONFIRMED (ps + logs) | fixed 508d0c8aa + 1a8faa545 + ed5a000ea + cf6157d03 + 856c0abe5 · LIVE-PROVEN with Q-241/242 on 3.0.66 06:36 | Cause, measured: at the 22:07 quit the app ran neither the window `closed` nor will-quit (main.log: no 'Terminating goose serve' for that session; goosed's log: no 'stop requested' until the 22:11 per-pid kill, turn_assessment still running 4 s after the new app started), and goosed had no way to notice; separately will-quit was `async` (Electron never awaits it: 28 'Terminating' vs 1 exit line in main.log) so no quit ever waited. Fix: (1) goosed runs `serve --exit-when-stdin-closes` on a stdin pipe the app never writes — the kernel closes it however the app ends, goosed runs its SIGTERM teardown (tailscaled per pid) and exits 129 (real binary: 6 ms); (2) will-quit holds the quit until every goosed EXITS (stops already under way included), SIGKILL per the derived GOOSED_SIGKILL_AFTER_MS only after goosed's own teardown had its turn; (3) at launch the app reaps, per pid on proof (ppid 1 · this uid · argv = THIS bundle's goose + ` serve ` desktop shape · identity re-read before each signal), goosed an earlier run left — SIGTERM so its teardown frees the mesh; other bundles' orphans logged, untouched; (4) Link's MeshEngine::start stops, per pid on proof (ppid 1 · this uid · argv ends with exactly our tailscaled argv), a tailscaled whose goosed is DEAD, then connects; a live goosed's daemon is never touched (Connect says 'quit that goose'). Live prove: quit via install.sh with Link connected → no `goose serve`/tailscaled left with ppid 1; relaunch → Link reconnects with no click |

Docs: DESIGN-NODES §6.4 + J3 step 3 (across windows = the in-process wait; the cross-process holders are proven by J6 and a chat on a CLI `goose serve`), DESIGN-SESSION-LOOPS limit 9 / D7 / D9 / §5.4. | Q-298 | 2026-09-28 | MISLEADS | an answered question stays 'Needs you' in five places | Live critic on 3.0.68 (/Users/mihaiperdum/goose-builds/quality/CRITIC-2026-09-28-3.0.68): The ask_user question 'An inactive project lead — which decision…' (09:08) told the model 'Their answer will arrive as their next message'; the user's next message 5 s later settled it, yet 70 min later it is still Needs you on the top pill, Active now, the sidebar row, the engine card and the chat card (needs_you.rs:113; the only resolve path acp/server/needs_you.rs:193) | a user answers a goose question by typing instead of clicking | CONFIRMED (critic screenshots) | fixed 91d2ee2ff · awaiting live prove | when the user sends a message while a question is open, mark it superseded (or ask 'does this answer it?') and tell the model which happened · fixed 2026-09-28 91d2ee2ff (+ SDK f35f9ae5c): the person's own plain message (ACP on_prompt, not a loop tick, not a slash command) closes every open question of that chat as the new status SUPERSEDED with superseded_by = the message (needs_you::supersede_open), stores an agent-only note just before the message, then tells the loop (Superseded holds it like an answer; the next tick reads 'they did not answer it and wrote instead: "…"'). The model reads: 'Your question "<q>" was still open on the person's card when they sent the message below instead of answering there. It is now closed as superseded by that message: not answered from the card, and not dismissed. Their message: "<text>" / Read their words: if they settle it, go on with that; if they do not and you still cannot proceed without an answer, ask again with ask_user.' ask_user's instructions, description and result now say the next message answers on the card or supersedes it. Design vs anthropics/claude-code#88850: a distinct superseded signal, not keep-open (goose ends the turn at ask_user, so keep-open IS this bug) and not 'dismissed' (it manufactures a refusal). All five surfaces derive from the one store's needsYou = the engine's open items, so they clear on the next session_activity read. Tests: acp_needs_you_superseded_test (the OpenAI mock answers only when the request carries the note + quoted message), needs_you unit tests, runner a_superseded_question_holds_the_loop_like_an_answer_and_quotes_the_message, needsYouSuperseded.test.tsx (five surfaces open → clear). PROVE: ask_user in a chat, then type a reply in the composer instead of the card — every Needs you clears within one activity read and the model's next turn treats the text as the reply or re-asks. |

(1) the app's real ring was dead too — headless Chromium on the compiled main.css: the FOCUS token (`outline-none focus-visible:outline-2 …outline-ring`) computes to `outline: none 0px` on Tab, because outline-none sets `--tw-outline-style: none` and outline-2 draws `outline-style: var(--tw-outline-style)` — every lz primitive (122 FOCUS uses), ui/button.tsx and StartLoopDialog had no ring; FOCUS + both literals gain `focus-visible:outline-solid` → `solid 2px rgb(29,78,216)` (dark rgb(59,130,246)). (2) the five `ring-border-active` sites (title trigger, JSON-tree string button + key toggle, rename input, SessionListView nostr textarea) carry FOCUS; ConfirmationModal's dead ring dropped (the Button's outline is the ring). SWEEP: a TS-AST scan of every className / cx-cn-clsx-cva argument / class-named binding through candidatesToCss found 664 sites naming a utility with no CSS: font-medium/semibold/bold/normal (224 — the MCP registration's `initial` deletes the keys; re-registered inline 400-700 in main.css, since font-lz-* is dropped by tailwind-merge inside cn() beside font-mono, measured), the older palette (textStandard/textSubtle/borderSubtle/bg-background-default/text-text-muted …) remapped by upstream's own rename maps (0e49cc7b9, cb42cf4ad), shadcn names never registered (text-foreground, bg-muted, border-border, text-destructive …), faded washes → the solid step, decorative shadows deleted (overlays take shadow-lz-overlay), text-md → text-base, animate-indeterminate → animate-lz-indeterminate; ui/Pill.tsx and ui/Dot.tsx (imported by nothing) deleted. GUARD: lz/classesCompile.test.ts (+ lz/sourceClasses.ts) fails on any class-context token that names a utility and compiles to nothing, and on any class string that clears the outline and draws one it never styles; a fixture proves both. LIVE PROVE: the next packaged build — Tab to the session title: a solid blue 2px ring; Tab through any Studio view (Segmented, Button, Checkbox): each shows the ring; onboarding/recipe/settings labels read medium/semibold. |

| Q-341 | 2026-09-28 | DEAD END (owner) | a needs-you card cannot be answered while a prompt is being read | Owner: 'while prompt reading is happening I can't select a thing in there … we either need to allow users to go forward or do something about these need you' — the options, the recommended answer and the text box are disabled while the chat's turn is busy | any open question while the chat's turn runs (long prompt reads on the split take minutes) | CONFIRMED (owner) | fixed 31cb7a8a3 (i18n 911278622) · awaiting live prove · on main 838b93b62 | a pick or typed answer while the turn is busy is accepted and QUEUED like a queued message ('Queued · answers when this turn ends'), cancellable; Q-298 (on main after 3.0.69) already closes an open question as superseded when the person types in the composer · fixed 2026-09-28: answering at once is NOT safe — the resolve does not touch the turn, but the answer reaches the model only as the next chat message and useChatSession.handleSubmit silently refuses one mid-turn (Answered on the engine, words nowhere). So a pick/typed answer mid-turn is QUEUED (needsYouAnswerQueue.ts): a solid violet 'Queued · answers when this turn ends' row with the words and Cancel (the words go back to the box; a re-pick replaces). Dismiss sends nothing, so it closes at once. ORDER at turn end: every queued answer resolved oldest first, then ONE message with all of them, then the composer's queue (ChatInput queueHeld holds it from the queueing until the answer's turn has started) — composer first would supersede the answered question (Q-298) and the answer would be refused; ChatInput.needsYouOrder.test.tsx proves both the order and the hazard without the hold. The tick door counts queued answers. A question closed while its answer waited, or a failed save, shows a solid red 'Not sent' notice with the words. PROVE: during a long prompt read pick the recommended answer and type a composer message — the card says Queued; when the turn ends the answer turn runs first, then the message; the question clears. |

| Q-343 | 2026-09-28 | STABILITY (hygiene) | 2,684 unfinished llm_request.<uuid>.jsonl files pile up in the log folder | ~/.local/state/goose/logs holds 2,684 llm_request.<uuid>.jsonl files back to 03:57 — calls that never completed and were never renamed into the numbered rotation (cancelled side calls?) — never cleaned; the harness also counts only the numbered files (r1.mjs pollCalls) and logged no calls for #3p turn 8, which made tool calls (sessions.db 770697/770700) | every cancelled or interrupted call | CONFIRMED (ls) | fixed ccc804927 · awaiting live prove | Cause (measured on the 2,683 files): a request log is renamed into the rotation only by its handle's Drop. Every in-process ending drops it — a cancelled stream included — except a panic (Drop returned early while panicking); the pile comes from exits that drop nothing: `goose serve`'s stop ends in std::process::exit after its teardown, and a SIGKILL after the quit grace. Every burst is stamped seconds before a teardown: 8 fact-check calls last written 03:57:03 local, cut mid-body (BufWriter unflushed — 626 of the files are 0 bytes), CLI log 20260928_001742 'goose serve: teardown complete; exiting' (exit 129) at 00:57:08Z, the next goose at 03:57:11; none after 03:57, oldest June 25. Fix (providers/utils.rs): in-flight name carries the writer (llm_request.<pid>.<uuid>.jsonl); Drop always ends the log (rotated, or removed and named — never left); every open log is registered and `end_request_logs_at_exit` gives each a last {"error":"goose exited (SIGTERM) with this request in flight"} line and rotates it right before goose serve's process::exit; `sweep_request_log_leftovers` at startup (goose serve/CLI, goosed) removes only the writer's own in-flight names, only files last written before this process's request log began, and a named writer's only when that pid is gone or started after the write — numbered rotation and other files untouched. Harness r1.mjs: a numbered file is a finished call, counted by its last line (usage, error or no-usage — the old reader skipped no-usage calls forever) in a new `ended` column of calls.tsv, polled while the view is away and once more at each turn's end. Tests: cancelled stream (dropped while waiting) rotated with exactly what it wrote; panic unwinding rotates; rotation failure removes; exit ends two in-flight logs with the cause as last line; the sweep on the 03:57 shape; naming; liveness. LIVE PROVE: install, start the app once → the logs folder holds no llm_request.<uuid>.jsonl older than that start (the sweep's info line in the CLI log names the count); quit the app mid-turn (a fact check in flight) → after quit no llm_request.<pid>.<uuid>.jsonl remains and llm_request.0's last line is the exit's error; relaunch → nothing left over |
| Q-344 | 2026-09-28 | MISLEADS | answering one of two open questions on its card closes the other as superseded | Found by the Q-340/341 fix, read in code: the card's answer goes to the model as a plain chat message (NeedsYouCard onAnswer → BaseChat sendAnswer → on_prompt), and on_prompt closes EVERY open question of the chat as superseded by any plain message (crates/goose/src/acp/server.rs:3014-3020, Q-298) — so with two cards open, Answer on card 1 makes card 2 vanish and the model reads 'still open … they sent the message below instead of answering there' about a question the person had not got to. Answers queued mid-turn are safe (they are resolved first and go as one message); the idle path is not | any chat with 2+ open ask_user questions, answered one at a time | CONFIRMED (reproduced on the real websocket harness: with the mark ignored, the second question is Superseded) | fixed d56c59d89 (schema 7c769cbce) · awaiting live prove · on main 838b93b62 | mark the card-answer message and skip the supersede for it · fixed 2026-09-28: THE MARK is `_meta.goose.needsYouAnswers = [item ids]` on the session/prompt. SET by the desktop on both answer paths — NeedsYouCard onAnswer (immediate: resolve, then sendAnswer(text, [item.id])) and needsYouAnswerQueue deliverQueuedAnswers (Q-341's batch: every resolve, then ONE sendAnswer(text, [ids…])) — through BaseChat → UserInput.needsYouAnswers → useChatSession.handleSubmit → submitMessage options.meta (acp/prompt.ts needsYouAnswersMeta). READ in on_prompt (server.rs, the Q-298 branch) via GooseAcpAgent::is_card_answer → needs_you::card_answer_ids + take_card_answers. FORGED/STALE: the mark is honoured only when EVERY id is a question of this chat that is Answered (resolved on the card), the message text carries its recorded answer, and no earlier message delivered it — answer_delivered_at is stamped in the same transaction, so a mark works once. Anything else (open/superseded/dismissed/unknown id, a typed message borrowing an answered id, a second use, a malformed mark) writes nothing, logs a warn with the reason, and the message supersedes as typed. What a bad mark could still do: a message that really contains an answered-but-undelivered question's words dodges the supersede once — the other cards stay open (the person still sees and can answer them); it can never close or hide a question. A typed composer message has no mark and still supersedes (Q-298). TRACE: YES at 12:32:50, 0 → 40,361 of 44,053 (3,692 left to read). WAIT: `GET /goose/admission` on rank 0 (new, rank_admission.py) parks on a Condition until the watchdog reopens — no clock. goose maps a 503 with that code (never the prose) to `ProviderError::EngineHold`; `with_retry_config`/`retry_operation` wait it out via `engine_hold::wait_for_admission` (no timeout, no attempt counted); it ends at the lift, on Stop (the future drops), or when the engine goes away (a ServerError that says so). TURN LINE: progress status 'Waiting: <the watchdog's own reason>' (culprit-true — 'quitting other apps frees it' only when other apps took the memory), then 'Memory recovered: the engine admits new requests again — sending the request'; no UI change. | Q-400 | 2026-09-28 | PERFORMANCE | the swarm router reports queue_depth 55 on the split during one chat, and ~8 requests to 8091 retried at the same instant | #3r goose log 2026-09-28 14:10:31 'pick … free_slots 8, queued_ms 0, queue_depth 55' and at 14:18:31 about 8 requests retried together (Q-397 lane's note) — one chat should have 1 agent call + a handful of helpers (labels, fact checks, titles) in flight, not 55 queued | a long split chat with many tool calls | CONFIRMED (log words read, 2026-09-28: `queued` 0→77 with no matching pick for every dropped waiter; 'yielded to a user turn' 1, 2, 3 … 19 per wave) | fixed f5b4a500c+d8cdff027 · awaiting live prove | TWO causes. (1) the count leaked: `Router::queue_on` (swarm_router.rs) took a waiter back only after its wait returned, so every reviewer dropped mid-wait by a user turn stayed counted — the 14:10:31 pick read 'free_slots 8 … queue_depth 55', impossible for a real queue; now a drop guard (`Waiting`) releases it on every exit. (2) the real pile: the end-of-turn reviewer (answer check / memory review, turn_priority.rs `after_user_turns`) re-asked after EVERY yield, and chat 20260928_21's next turn always came ~6 s after the last ended, so no reviewer ever finished and one more joined per turn — 18 calls at 13:42:43 (8 leased, 10 queued), all dropped at 13:42:47; 19 at 14:18:31 retrying against the memory-hold 503 (the '8 retried at once'). Now a reviewer is marked with its chat at the turn's end (`turn_priority::chat_mark`) and a newer user turn in THAT chat ends it ('superseded by a newer turn in its chat; not asked again') — its notice would land under the newer reply anyway; another chat's turn still only postpones it. Mechanism, no cap. Tests: `a_pick_dropped_while_it_waits_leaves_the_queue`, `a_chats_reviewers_do_not_pile_up_queue_ahead_of_its_turn_or_leak_the_depth` (19 turns, 8 slots), turn_priority's supersede + no-pile — each fails with its mechanism reverted. Tool-chain summaries were read too: one per chain, dedup'd, never queued — not the pile. Live prove: a long split chat's log shows at most one reviewer per idle gap and queue_depth back to 0 after every burst. |

Fix (rank 0 only, no spec flag — only the Mac that runs rank 0 needs the build): /v1/status reads every live socket without waiting before it counts, and num_running = rows someone still waits for + handlers with no row yet (departed / `leaving` rows listed, not counted); `still_wanted` in `settle` and the hold drops a departed request unshared (phase `queued`, RANK_ADMISSION `dropped_departed`), then takes the next arrival at once; one `client_departure` names it once, stops the context and wakes the handler; DepartureContext stops a row whose handler already ended. No clock. NOT fixed, by nature: the prompt step already running (MLX's forward is not interruptible) — the chat's call still waits for it (#3r: ~36 s, ~9 s). Trace: both picks free_slots 0→8; the chat's call admitted ≈5.7 s / ≈6.4 s earlier. Tests: `a_client_that_leaves_during_prefill_frees_its_slot_before_the_step_ends` (fails pre-fix: num_running 4; fails with either admission check removed), Q-231's test now expects 1 not 3. Live prove: on the next split chat with dropped reviewers, the pick after a user turn reads free_slots > 0, the rank-0 log shows `dropped_departed` for a held one, and no CANCELLED_BY_CLIENT with phase `prefill` and `prefilled` ≥ one chunk for a request that was held. |

On timeout, cancel (future dropped, or the call's token cancelled) and goose serve's teardown (new FIRST step 'shell commands'), process_groups::CommandProcesses SIGTERMs the group only under goose_sidecar::owns_process_group's proof (getpgid(leader)==leader, leader!=getpgrp()), falls back per pid when it fails, and SIGKILLs confirmed survivors per pid after EXIT_GRACE (the old 500 ms drain window, now one home); a terminal-group command is signalled per pid over the shell and its descendants, never killpg. Linux: the cause says plainly it cannot be determined (no `sample`). BREAK (independent refuter): a server the model starts with `&` used to die with the desktop's quit signal to goosed's group and would now outlive the app — fixed: an own-group command that ENDS with members left is recorded with each member's `ps lstart`, and the teardown ends it per pid only while a stamped member proves the group id was not reused (tests: backgrounded child recorded + ended by the teardown; a forged stamp is left alone). Residual: Q-407 |
| Q-407 | 2026-09-28 | STABILITY | a goosed that dies WITHOUT its teardown (crash, or the desktop's SIGKILL fallback) now leaves in-flight shell commands running | Q-406 moved goose serve's shell commands into process groups of their own; before, they sat in goosed's group, which gooseServe.ts killGroupOrProcess signals with kill(-pid). goosed's SIGTERM/SIGHUP/stdin-EOF path stops them (teardown step 'shell commands', first), but a SIGKILLed or crashed goosed runs no teardown, and the desktop's group signal no longer reaches them — a TCC-blocked find would then outlive goosed as before Q-406 | goosed crash or SIGKILL fallback while a shell command runs | CONFIRMED (reproduced: a stand-in goosed SIGKILLed mid-command with no watchdog — 'the in-flight command outlived its killed goosed') | fixed (this commit) · awaiting live prove | BUILT: goose serve spawns `goose shell-watchdog` (hidden subcommand) first thing at startup, in a process group of its own (the desktop's kill(-pid) of goosed never reaches it), stdin = a pipe whose write end only goosed holds (close-on-exec). process_groups tells it every group the teardown would stop — `live <leader> <stamp>` at spawn, `ended <leader> pid=stamp…` for a command that ended with members left (the `&` server, Q-406), `gone <leader>` when a group stops being goosed's (the teardown sends it only AFTER signalling, so a goosed killed mid-teardown leaves the rest to the watchdog). However goosed ends, the kernel closes the pipe and the watchdog reads EOF — no heartbeat, no clock decides goosed is gone; a read error that is not EOF ends NOTHING (it says nothing about goosed) and says so. On EOF it ends each group through Q-406's terminate_command_groups, only under proof: live — the leader's start time still equals its stamp (a pid/pgid handed to another process fails), then goose_sidecar::owns_process_group before the killpg; live but the leader already GONE (independent refuter's case: a member's next write to goosed's closed pipe SIGPIPEs it, bash exits before the watchdog looks, a `&` server stays) — the group's current members that started no earlier than the recorded leader, per pid; ended — a stamped member still that process and still in the group, then per pid. The watchdog is dispatched before the CLI's project tracker and command counter (it is goose serve's helper, not a use of goose); development-gates.md §4 now names signal_owned_group's caller set. SIGTERM → EXIT_GRACE → SIGKILL survivors is the teardown's own escalation (it picks the signal, never whether anything ends). Stamps are now the kernel's (macOS proc_pidinfo PROC_PIDTBSDINFO sec.usec, Linux /proc stat field 22, `ps lstart` elsewhere): every own-group command is stamped as it spawns, `ps` measured 3.5 ms per call, and lstart is one-second-coarse. MEASURED before choosing: a registry FILE read by the desktop covers only a desktop-started goosed and needs the proof re-homed in TypeScript over `ps` (Node has no getpgid); a watcher INSIDE each command's group breaks a bare `wait` (a 0.2 s job still waiting after 3 s) and keeps every group alive; the orphan-goosed reap (Q-223) and clean.sh's classes prove processes by command line, which a shell command lacks. TESTS (shell_watchdog.rs, shell.rs): a stand-in goosed (this test binary as a child) arms the watchdog exactly as serve does, runs `sleep … &` then `sleep … \| cat`, is SIGKILLed → both gone (FAILS with the arm call removed: 'the in-flight command outlived its killed goosed'); the recovery on recorded lines ends a live pipeline group and an ended group while a process holding a recorded pid with a different start time (the reused-pgid shape, live and ended) and a `gone` group stay alive (FAILS with the end skipped and with the stamp check dropped — the impostor is signalled); the same test ends a server left in a group whose leader died after goosed, and leaves a member that started BEFORE its recorded leader (FAILS with the leader-gone branch removed, and with its start-time bound dropped); a pipe error before EOF ends nothing; clap parses `goose shell-watchdog` by the constant serve spawns. REAL BINARY: `goose shell-watchdog` fed a live record for `sleep|cat` + a forged record for an unrelated sleep, stdin closed → exit 0 in 0.60 s, 'SIGTERM to 1 running shell command group(s) [60006] … left alone … [60007]', group empty, impostor alive. goose serve itself was not launched (its startup reaper would touch processes this session did not start) — its arm is one call before any other spawn. GATE: fmt, goose --lib 2125/0, acp_* 17 binaries 0 failed, goose-cli 943/0 + goose-mcp 98/0 (NPM_CONFIG_PREFIX unset for the test process), clippy goose/goose-cli/goose-server -D warnings clean, development_gates 11/0, wincheck HEAD exit 0; UI untouched. INDEPENDENT REFUTER: lenses EOF delivery, pid reuse, gate 4, protocol/locks, regressions (II-7 detached groups send `gone` and survive; a normal quit and a desktop restart end nothing that should survive; orphanGoosedReap matches only ` serve`) — no defect survived; its two LOW findings are fixed above. Residual: a watchdog killed on its own is logged once (`shell_watchdog_gone`) and not respawned; `goose run` / the swarm process arm no watchdog |

(1) engine hold :157 / acp_ws :478 — TEST: the three tests share one scripted model whose admission counter is cumulative and the serial lock grants them in any order; the hold test asserted absolute counts (== 1, == 2) while its siblings counted from waits_before. With stop_during_a_hold first (count left at 1) `== 2` held the instant the FIRST hold's GET landed, before the admitted re-send (left 1 right 2), or `== 1` never held (the :478 timeout). Every failing run's order was any_other -> stop_during -> a_hold. Fix: until_waiting takes the target count, both hold tests count from waits_before; no assertion loosened. Harness race found on the way: Model counted an admission wait under one mutex and parked its socket under another, so an admit() between them answered nobody — now one lock. Loops: before 22/30 (6 at :157, 2 at :478); after 30/30 + 20/20 with stop_during before a_hold in all 20. (2) needs-you :94 overflow — a PRODUCT race, filed Q-421 (the person's message sorted before its superseded note when a second ticked between them); fixed in on_prompt. Loops: natural before 99/100 (1 overflow), after 100/100; forced second boundary (temporary 1.1 s delay before the note, never committed) before 0/10, after 10/10. (3) link control_service :184 — TEST: the Offline check was `!last_poll_error.contains("401")` and the transport error carries the stub's ephemeral PORT in its URL; a port like 40117/54012/33401 (~0.6% of Linux's range, ~0.2% of macOS's) held it false forever over a correct Offline row (CI: every other test done 13 s before the deadline, a refused dial fails at once, 100 ms poll cadence). DEADLINE 15 s = 150 polls and 3x the poll's own 5 s bound — not the cause, kept. Fix: Offline with a transport error naming `:{stub_port}/v1/swarm/` and not the answered class (' answered ', 'token mismatch'). Loops: stub on a 401-bearing port (temporary hack) before 0/3 with the CI's exact message, after 10/10; natural full binary before 100/100 (the odds are too low to hit in 100 on macOS — the forced port is the proof), after 100/100. Gate: fmt, the three binaries, goose --lib, leanzero-link, clippy -D warnings (goose, leanzero-link), development_gates, wincheck HEAD — all green |
